486. HANNAH SAVORY and JANE FERGUSON were indicted for stealing, on the 16th of November, 1 gown, value 5s., and 1 shawl, value 5s., the goods of Hannah Lake, widow.
HANNAH LAKE. I am a widow, and live in Kensington poor-house. I lost a gown and shawl on the 16th of November, from a room on the two-pair of stairs: the prisoner was in the house; I had seen my articles in their place on the Saturday, and missed them on Monday; I stopped Ferguson on the stairs, and said I suspected she knew something about my shawl and gown - they had been in a box, which was corded.
Prisoner FERGUSON. She seized me by the throat, and said, "I insist upon searching you;" I said she was not a proper person - she said if I would not be searched she would have my life: she then searched me, and took me to the watch-house; she said she would have many a pound out of me, or she would send me across the herring-pond. Witness. I did not - she told me where they were pawned.
JOHN KILLINGWORTH. I am a pawnbroker. These articles were pawned with me on the 17th of November, by Ferguson, I believe.
Prisoner FERGUSON. Q. Do not you remember telling the Magistrate it was not me? A. No; I told him I believed it was you; I had some doubts about the other - two persons came, and one stood near the door.
WILLIAM BENNETT. The prosecutrix came to me, and said the two prisoners had confessed where the things were - I went and took them, but found no duplicate on them.(Property produced and sworn to.)
FERGUSON's Defence. The Magistrate asked if she could swear it was me - she said she could not; there were sixteen people in the same room; the pawnbroker said he thought it was a girl taller than me, and the Magistrate discharged me; I had not gone ten yards, when I was taken to Hammersmith, and locked up for fourteen days, and then the pawnbroker said, to the best of his knowledge, it was me.
WILLIAM BENNETT. I generally take my prisoners to Bow-street: the Magistrate asked me if one of the overseers were come - I said, No; he said I might take them to Kensington, which I did, but they were never out of my charge.
FERGUSON - GUILTY. Aged 16.
Transported for Seven Years.
SAVORY - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18290115-219]
487. WILLIAM PROUD was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of December, 28 pieces of wood, value 18s., the goods of Thomas Cubitt and Lewis Cubitt; and SAMUEL COOPER was indicted for feloniously receiving the same, knowing them to have been stolen.
MR. CLARKSON conducted the prosecution.
WILLIAM WOODBERRY. In consequence of information, I went to Proud's lodging, in Augusta-court, Laurence-street, Chelsea, about eight or nine o'clock in the morning of the 10th December: he had been apprehended the day before - I was informed in the house that was his room, and I found some pieces of wood in a box, which his wife unlocked - I believe they are called beadings or mouldings; in the kitchen in which Cooper lived, I found some mahogany; in a cellar under the stairs I found a large quantity of wood chopped up for fire-wood - I found these three pieces in a closet in the parlour; Cooper was not at home, but I left word for him to come to Mr. Cubitt's - he came, I left word for him to come to Mr. Cuasked him about the property - he said he had the mahogany from a lodger of his, who owed him money; I think he mentioned the name, but I forget it - he said he had had it several months; I think he said nine months; he said he did not know how these pieces got in the closet, but there was a piece of oak cill there which he had from the same man.
Cross-examined by MR. BODKIN. Q.Why did not you bring the cill here? A.Because we had a great deal to bring; we have not ascertained that it was not Mr. Cubitt's - I think Cooper came in about four hours after I left word; it is a single round house - there is but one kitchen.
ABRAHAM THOMPSON WOOD. I am in the service of Messrs. Cubitt's, the builders - Proud was in their service as a labourer, and Cooper was in their service till within three weeks or a month; I swear to these two pieces of mahogany, as belonging to Messrs. Cubitt's, and these two pieces of ash I can swear to; Proud said he never stole a bit of wood before.
Cross-examined. Q.What are you? A. A clerk - I have nothing to do with manufacturing any wood; we did not miss any, but supposed wood to be stolen at different times - I can match these pieces of mahogany with some on the premises; I am certain that these pieces formed part of another plank cut from the same log as this plank - the whole log was in Mr. Cubitt's possession, and has not been cut four months.
WILLIAM TAPSON. I am in the employ of Messrs. Cubitt's - I make barrows. I know these pieces of ash they were in my possession; these form the leg of a navigator's barrow - these were one solid body at one time; this piece I have brought was taken out here - they match exactly.
Prisoner PROUD. I know of nothing but the pieces of moulding, and the bits of ash - I took them to make hammer handles; they would not lend me their hammers. Witness. He never had leave to do that to my knowledge.
COURT. Q.When had you seen them on the premises? A.Never, till they were found at the prisoner's- I never saw them at my master's.
JOHN TIPPETT. I am a joiner, in the employ of Messrs. Cubitt's. I have some moulding planes, fromwhich I believe these pieces of moulding were formed - they fit exactly.
WILLIAM DENTRY. I am a sawyer to Messrs. Cubitt's. I sawed up this mahogany from a log on the 20th of August; these small pieces belonged to the same log as this plank does, I have no doubt.
MR. BODKIN called -
JOHN BULL. I am a carpenter. I lodged in Cooper's house in the summer and autumn of 1827; I left various tools, a piece of oak, and various woods for rent which I owed him.
JURY. Q. Did you leave any mahogany? A. I left some mahogany and cedar, and various woods; I was in the habit of jobbing about, and using all sorts of woods at different houses in Chelsea - mahogany is so similar, it is impossible to swear to it.
JURY to ABRAHAM THOMSON WOOD. Q.Are any of the labourers allowed to take firewood? A.No, not on any occasion.
Proud received a good character.
PROUD - GUILTY.
Recommended to Mercy - Confined Six Months.
COOPER - NOT GUILTY
[trialAccount t18290115-220]
488. JOSEPH TIPLING & HENRY SLATTER were indicted for stealing, on the 21st of December, 1 counterpane, value 15s., the goods of William Puttock.
WILLIAM PUTTOCK. I am a gardener. On the 19th of December this counterpane hung in my garden, close by the pleasure-ground of Mr. Griffin, at Hampstead, to dry; two little girls told me they saw a man get over the pales, and wrap something up - I went out, and found it on Tipling, between the Load of Hay and the George public-houses; there were two or three others by him - I took him, and Slatter ran away; he was taken on the Friday night - I swear he was one who ran away; Tippling said the man (meaning Slatter) said he would beat him if he did not carry the counterpane - that was not said in Slatter's presence; I told Slatter what he had said, and he denied it; I had seen it safe twenty minutes before.
WILLIAM GREEN. Tipling was given into my charge - he told me two boys and a man were with him, but he did not know his name; I got information from a boy in the Regent's-park, who took me to Slatter's; as soon as I went into his room he said what was I searching for -I said a waistcoat; he said, "I know nothing about that, but I know all about a quilt, let me finish my breakfast, and I will go - when he got to the office he said, "You have got it, have you;" I said, "Whose handkerchief is this?" he said mine.(Properly produced and sworn to.)
SLATTER's Defence. I found the counterpane by the side of the trees.
SLATTER - GUILTY. Aged 18.
Confined Four Months.
TIPLING - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18290115-221]
489. PATRICK TEAGAN was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of January, 1 coat, value 5s., the goods of Jeremiah Crawley.
JEREMIAH CRAWLEY. I am a labourer. I left a coat at my lodging on Monday last; I went down to Greenwich, was sent for on Thursday, and the coat was gone- this is it.
MARGARET DELAY. The prosecutor slept at our house, in Brown Bear-alley, on Sunday night, and went to Greenwich on Monday. The prisoner slept there two nights, and on Wednesday he went out with Crawley's coat on; I went up stairs, and the coat was gone - I came down, and opened the street door; he was a good way down the court - I called him, and he came back; I said he had got the young man's coat on - he said he had not; I said he had, and sent for the officer, but before the officer came he pulled it off.
THOMAS HAYLOCK. The witness sent for me. I took the prisoner; I asked him how he came to steal it - he said he thought the devil was in him for the moment, but if I would let him go he would enlist for a soldier.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I was in liquor.
THOMAS HAYLOCK. He was not in liquor; the girl who detected him said he had it under his own jacket, and the tail of it tucked up.
GUILTY. Aged 22.
Confined Four Months.
[trialAccount t18290115-222]
490. JOHN WILLIAMS was indicted for stealing, on the 9th of December, 1 waistcoat, value 13s., the goods of Anthony Rawlings.
ANTHONY RAWLINGS. I am a tailor, and live in Tottenham-court-road. I went out on the 9th of December, about eleven o'clock, and left the waistcoat safe - I came back in the evening, and it was gone; the prisoner came to my shop on the 24th, and asked the price of a waistcoat that was in the window; I said 10s.; my wife came in and said."That is the man who stole the waistcoat;" I said, "Are you quite sure?" - she said Yes; I called my apprentice Tucker, and asked if he knew the young man - he said,"Yes, that is the young man;" I took him into the parlour, and said I should send for an officer - he wanted to get away, and offered to leave 1s. in part of payment, and then his watch, but I would not allow it.
Prisoner. His wife wanted me to leave the watch, and I would not. Witness. No, be offered it himself.
JANE RAWLINGS. On the 9th of December, the prisoner came to our shop, and asked if we had any kneebreeches; I said No - he said, "What time will your husband be at home?" I said, "Four o'clock;" he said he would come at half-past four - I went into the parlour, and he came and drew the waistcoat from under the gaslamp; I went after him, but I had to get another person first, and could not overtake him.
Prisoner. She gave me a box on the ear. Witness. No, I laid hold of him, and said he was the boy.
RICHARD TUCKER. I had seen the prisoner twice before, on that day: I was at work when he came.
Prisoner's Defence. Is it likely I should go and steal a waistcoat, and then go to buy another in a fortnight; it does not stand to reason.
GUILTY. Aged 16.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18290115-223]
491. JAMES HART was indicted for stealing, on the 6th of December, 5 sovereigns; 1 bag, value 1d.; 2 half-sovereigns; 5 half-crowns, and 4s., the property of James Bonner, from his person.
JAMES BONNER. I am a labourer. I had this money in a bag, in my right-hand waistcoat pocket on the 6th of December; the prisoner and I went into a public-house, and had a drop of beer - I happened to fall asleep; I do not know that I had been drinking too much - I went in there between one and two o'clock, and awoke between five and six; the prisoner and my money were then gone - I knew him before; he is a labourer - I went to look after him, but could not find him, till the next night, which was Sunday; I cannot think of the name of the place - Mr. Cooper took him, and found some money on him; I charged him with taking it, and he delivered up the money he had left, and did not say he had not taken it.
Prisoner. Q.Did not you sleep with me on Saturday night? A. Yes, and asked him for my money; he used very bad language.
WILLIAM BOARD. I am a labourer. I was having a pint of beer, between nine and ten o'clock; the prisoner came, and asked me if I would take 1l. 11s. to his daughter - I received from him a sovereign, a half-sovereign, and 1s.; he said he was going on a tramp - he has a son, who has lost the use of his limbs, and I was to give this to his daughter, while she was in trouble; on the Monday she came and had the sovereign, and said her father was in trouble.
JOSEPH COOPER. I am an officer. I found the prisoner in a room in Johnson's-place, sitting on the floor, in a state of intoxication - I asked what he had done with Bonner's money; he said, "I have got it;" I said,"Produce it" - he pulled out three sovereigns, a half-sovereign, and 7s. in other money; the next morning, his daughter came - I got this sovereign from her.
Prisoner's Defence. When he came to my house, he asked for me; he slept with me that night - we went and had something to drink, and he never came near me till the Sunday afternoon; I ran from place to place to find him; he gave me this money, with his own hand, as he was intoxicated.
JURY to JAMES BONNER. Q.Did you give him the money? A. I cannot say.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18290115-224]
492. CHARLES SWAN and JAMES HICKEY were indicted for stealing, on the 20th of December, 1 waistcoat, value 8s., the goods of William Rabbits.
SARAH RABBITS. I am mother of William Rabbits - we live at Bedfont. On the 20th of December, this waistcoat was stolen from a back-yard; I had seen it three quarters of an hour before it was taken - on Friday, the day before the loss, the prisoners came and asked me if I could tell them where they could lay down on a little clean straw; I told them to go to the ostler's, who I thought would give them a lodging - this is the waistcoat.
Cross-examined by J. ALLEY. Q.Where was the waistcoat? A.On a line; my son William is eighteen years of age - I did not see them take it; on the Saturday, they came and stood talking to the same young man they had before; he gave me information - I live next door to the Bell public-house, at Bedfont.
JOHN EMERSON. I took up the prisoners, near the Wellington public-house, about two miles from the prosecutor's; I found the waistcoat in Hickey's pocket - he said next morning that he took it, but he wished he had had nothing to do with it.
Cross-examined. Q.Did not Hickey say he would rather pay for it than go to Newgate? A. Not in my hearing.
HICKEY - GUILTY. Aged 15.
Recommended to Mercy by the Jury, having a good character.
Confined Fourteen Days.
SWAN - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18290115-225]
493. BRIDGET McQUAIN was indicted for stealing, on the 18th of December, 4s., the money of Anthony Rawlings, from the person of John Rawlings .
JOHN RAWLINGS. I am the brother of Anthony Rawlings, a tailor, who lives in Tottenham-court-road: he gave me 7s. on the 18th of December - I was going by Torrington-square, where I saw the prisoner begging; I gave her 1d., and walked on - I had not gone far, before she came after me, and said, "I say, young man," and put her hand into my waistcoat pocket: I knocked it out, and asked her for my money - she said, "What money?" that she had no money belonging to me - I followed her, found the watchman, and gave charge of her; it was about half-past eight o'clock.
Prisoner. Q.Did not you give me 1s. besides the 1d.? A. No, I had no 1s.
ANTHONY RAWLINGS. I sent my brother to receive 1l. 13s., in Torrington-square: I gave him 7s. - he came back with 4s. short.
JOHN MURPHY. I am a watchman. The lad came and told me to lay hold of that woman, as she had robbed him of 4s. 6d.; I took another woman - he said, "No, this was the woman;" she ran into a dark passage, came out, and said, "Do you want me?" I took her to the watchhouse - she had 1s. 2d, on her; the constable told me to go back, and I found 4s. 6d. at the further end of the passage.
Prisoner. This watchman and my husband had a bit of a quarrel last Christmas-day, and he has had a spite against me ever since; he put my husband and two sister-in-law in the watchhouse. Witness. I have no spite against them; they are very quarrelsome - they are a set of tinkers.
JURY. Q.Is it a thoroughfare where you picked up the money? A. I understand nobody lives in the house; it is indicted - the door is open at all hours.
JURY to JOHN RAWLINGS. Q.How came you to stop and speak to her? A. I did not; she asked me to buy laces - I pulled out my silver and halfpence, and gave her a penny.
Prisoner's Defence. I went out to get the price of my sister's lodging; I got 1s. 2 1/2d., and was going home, this boy came and said, "Give me my money:" I said, "What money do you mean?" we passed four watchmen before he gave me in charge - there was another woman on the other side of the way; he said at first he that did give me the shilling - the watchman knows it.
JOHN MURPHY. No, he did not; he gave her in charge to me in Church-street - she ran into Bainbridge-street.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18290115-226]
494. THOMAS PRATT and WILLIAM DEADDROSS were indicted for stealing, on the 15th of December, 1 handkerchief, value 4s., the goods of George Richard Pooley, from his person.
GEORGE RICHARD POOLEY. I am clerk to a wine-merchant. On the 15th of December I was at the entrance of Holborn-court, Gray's Inn, between nine and ten o'clock in the evening, and lost a silk handkerchief; Roberts told me it was taken and I missed it: I did not see the prisoners then, but did in about ten minutes.
ARTAXERXAS BROWN. I am a constable of St. Andrew's parish. I was on duty on the night of the 15th, when the prisoners were brought to the watch-house; this handkerchief and some others were delivered to me - neither of the prisoners denied it.
BENJAMIN CHARLES MURRAY. I gave the five handkerchiefs to Brown: I took the prisoners into a shop, for they would not walk - in the scuffle Deaddross' hat fell off and I saw these handkerchiefs in it; this was between nine and ten o'clock at night, nearly opposite Brownlow-street; I had seen Pratt put his hand into the prosecutor's pocket, take the handkerchief, and give it to Deaddross, who was about twenty yards off when he took it.
THOMAS ROBERTS. I was in Holborn and saw the two prisoners and a third person; I saw Pratt go and take the prosecutor's handkerchief - I went and told him: I went with Murray, and found the prisoners in Holborn and took them.
THOMAS ASPREY. I am a watchman, and took the prisoners into custody; Deaddross said he had done nothing, but in the watch-house he owned one of the handkerchiefs.(Property produced and sworn to.)
DEADDROSS' Defence. I was coming down Holborn, and one handkerchief, which I owned to, was taken out of my hat.
PRATT put in a written Defence, stating that he had been seduced into crime. Several witnesses gave him a good character, and engaged to employ him.
PRATT - GUILTY. Aged 15.
Whipped and Discharged.
DEADDROSS - GUILTY. Aged 17.
Transported for Fourteen Years.

495. THOMAS WILLIAMS was indicted for stealing, on the 28th of December, 1 handkerchief, value 2s., the goods of Edward Wolfe Chadwick, from his person.
EDWARD WOLFE CHADWICK. On the 28th of December I was in Drury-lane. about half-past six o'clock in the evening, and saw the prisoner following me; I felt a jerk at my pocket, and on turning round, saw him putting my handkerchief into his bosom - I detained him and sent for the officer.
JAMES BIRCHALL. I am an officer and took the prisoner- he said he had hard work to get the handkerchief out; that he saw them eating some dinner in the cookshop, and he thought he would take the handkerchief to get some.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. He says he saw me following him, and I was looking into a cookshop; I saw his handkerchief hanging partly out of his pocket and it was pinned with this pin - I took hold of it to make him ashamed of himself for having it pinned; I saw the people eating and making mouths one at another, and I thought I would have some. GUILTY. Aged 19.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
Fifth Middlesex Jury - Before Mr. Sergeant Arabin.

496. WILLIAM PENEY was indicted for stealing, on the 4th of January, 16 yards of ribbon, value 15s., the goods of William Barnett.
WILLIAM BARNETT. I am a linen-draper, and live in Whitechapel-road. This ribbon is mine.
SAMUEL BUBB. I am errand-boy to Mr. Barnett. I was standing facing his window, to mind some baskets; I saw three lads round the window - the prisoner pulled out his hand, and had something in it; I ran after him, and a gentleman caught him just past the window - nothing was found on him; he threw it down, but I did not see him.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q.What were you doing? A.Minding some baskets - I had never seen him before, he had a hat on; I did not notice whether it was a hat or a cap; I looked at the other boys as well - the whole business took a minute or two; I did not loose sight of him - no one told me to say that it was not day-light; it was not a black cap he had on - it was a hat.
COURT. Q.Do you believe now, that he is the man? A. Yes, I know he is the man.
HANNAH ALLEN. I was going up the road and saw the ribbon lay; a woman took it up and was putting it under her cloak; I said to Mr. Barnett "Does this belong to you?" and the woman passed the ribbon from her hand to mine to give to the prosecutor; I turned round, and she was gone; I did not see the prisoner - they were running after him.
JAMES LEA. I am an officer. I heard Stop thief! called, and was sent for to take the prisoner, who was then in the shop.
Prisoner's Defence. What they have said is false, you may depend upon it; I was walking down Baker's-row, and was taken by two men who are not here.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18290115-229]
497. ELIZABETH GOODALL was indicted for stealing, on the 2d of January, 1 watch, value 2l.; 1 seal, value 6d.; and 1 watch-key, value 3d., the goods of Joseph Jones, from his person.
JOSEPH JONES. I am a journeymen carpenter On the 2d of January, between eleven and twelve o'clock at night, I was in High-street, Shadwell, going towards my home at Limehouse; the prisoner took hold of my arm, and asked me to accompany her home; I said No, but she kept by me all the length of the street - I said I have no money, I observed her notice my watch-ribbon, and put it out of sight, as I thought - we came to Love-lane and she said,"I won't! go any further with you; I want to speak to you" - I stopped, and she bolted from me all in a moment - I did not feel her take my watch, but it was gone; I ran after her - I fell down, got up, and ran again nearly a hundred yards; she fended me off with her left hand - I put out my arm, and she fell and smashed the watch down on the flags - I ran and got the watch, put it into the case, then ran and caught her; the watchman came up - I told him; I am sure I did not give her the watch. nor give her any encouragement - I am not married; I had had a female with me two or three minutes before,whom I expected to meet again, and it was not likely I should pick up this one.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Is the other lady here? A. No; she had made a mistake in the road, and had a young child to carry - she had been with me all the evening; I never saw the prisoner before she spoke to me: I fell on one of my knees in running after her - I had only one brace-button; there was but one button to that part of my trousers - the flap-button and brace-button were both one: that button-hole might slip off the button - I swear it was not unbuttoned when the watchman came up; she had run one hundred yards with the watch - it was between eleven and twelve o'clock; I had drunk a share of three pots of half-and-half.
JOHN BUDD. I am a watchman. About half-past eleven o'clock that night I saw the prisoner and the prosecutor together - he said,
"Will you take this woman?" I took hold of her; she said to me," Do call the gentlemen, and ask him to make it up, before I go to the watch-house" - I took her there, and gave charge of her; I saw the watch in Jones' hand - I cannot say whether his trousers were down.
Prisoner's Defence. I met this man in the highway -I asked him to go with me; we went up the court - I told him I would wish him a good night as there were passengers passing; I came out, and passed one watchman-the prosecutor came up and knocked me down; I shewed the mud at the watch-house.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18290115-230]
498. WILLIAM GREEN and THOMAS LINDSEY were indicted for stealing, on the 12th of January, 1 coat, value 5s., the goods of a person unknown.
JOB BRANCH. I am a gardener, and live at Maida-hill, Paddington. On the 12th of January I was in Lisson-grove, and met the two prisoners in company - they crossed the New-road and went down Stigo-lane; they then turned into Upper York-street, and went about forty yards to the left - there was a gig at the door of No.39; they crossed, looked into it, and went on towards Seymour-place- they appeared to speak to each other; Lindsey went up to the gig on the driving side, and took out a drab great coat - he turned the corner of Windham-place, and Green followed him; he threw the coat on his back, and they went on through several streets to George-street, Portman-square, Langham-place, Regent-street, and then turned into Oxford-street - as they went along they continued to look at the coat and to admire it; I met several patrols in Oxford-street - I spoke to them; Cost ran, and got ahead of them - they then cut across the road; I stopped them and said,
"You must not go any further" -Cost then came up and said,
"Where did you get that coat?" Lindsey said,
"I bought it; I can tell you where, but I shall not" - the coat belonged to a visitor, who is gone into the country.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. What are you? A. A florist; my garden is at Maida-vale-I went to the house the next morning; the proprietor said it was a gentleman who called, and he knew nothing of him - the coat was taken between five and six o'clock in the evening; they were taken about half-past six - I never prosecuted a person before in my life; the prisoners did nothing to me - I have no prejudice against them.
GEORGE Cost. I am a Bow-street patrol. I was on duty - Branch told me some persons had stolen a coat; I asked where it was, and he said they had got it on - I crossed the road, and they crossed; I returned, and took them into custody - I asked Lindsey where he got it; he said he had bought it - I took them to the office, and went in search of the owner; I found the house, but could not find him.
Cross-examined. Q. Did you search them? A. My brother officer did; there were 13s. and a watch found on them.
LINDSEY'S Defence. I was coming down the square, and met an elderly man dressed like a countryman who asked for relief; he had this coat, which he said he could not pawn and would sell it to me for 5s. - I put it on; it fitted me, and I gave him 5s. for it; I met this young man, and told him I had bought a coat - he was looking at it when these men came and took me.
GREEN'S Defence. I met this prisoner; he said he bought the coat - we were walking home.
GREEN - GUILTY. Aged 22.
LINDSEY - GUILTY. Aged 24.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18290115-231]
499. ALEXANDER WARREN was indicted for stealing, on the 23d of December, 1 hankerchief, value 2s., the goods of a man unknown, from his person.
RICHARD JONES. I am a jeweller. On the 23d of December, about half-past nine o'clock in the evening, my wife and I were going up Holborn, near Gray's Inn-lane - I saw the prisoner follow two gentlemen; I left my wife - I saw the prisoner take this handkerchief from a gentleman's pocket and attempt to put it into his own; I sprang on him and collared him - we struggled; he threw the handkerchief from him, and a gentleman took it up; I called my wife to go after the gentleman.
ELLEN CHARLOTTE JONES. I was with my husband - I saw the whole of the transaction; I went to the gentleman, who said he would have nothing to do with it.
GEORGE COLYER. I am a watchman, and took the prisoner.
Prisoner. Q. Did not two ladies and a gentleman come to the watch-house and say I was not the person? A. Yes; but they did not appear next day. The prisoner received an excellent character.
GUILTY. Aged 26.

[trialAccount t18290115-232]
500. MARY YOCKNEY was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of December, 1 watch, value 20s.; 1 watch-ribbon, value 1d., and 1 watch-key, value 2d., the goods of Nicholas Harvey, from his person.
NICHOLAS HARVEY. I met the prisoner on Sunday, the 14th of December, about half-past five o'clock, just by the corner of North-bank - she asked me to give her a few halfpence; I went to feel for my halfpence and missed my watch - I turned and cried Stop thief! two gentlemen came up; I saw the prisoner, and said she must walk with them - we could not find any officer, and took her down to High-street, Mary-le-bone: a gentleman found the watch in his garden - it was quite safe not above a minute before I saw the prisoner - she ran down North-green and I after her;this is the watch.
JAMES EDWARD MADDOX. I am in the coal trade, and live in North-bank, Lisson-grove. I heard a cry of Stop thief! I went out, and found two or three other neighbours, the prisoner and the prosecutor in dispute about a watch; the prisoner denied having it - the prosecutor spoke very positive as to her person, and wished to have her searched - she refused, but said she would go any where and he searched by a female - on the Thursday following, I found the watch in my garden, which is three or four doors off the spot.
JURY. Q.Was the altercation on this side of your garden? A. I should presume she had passed my garden - I found the prosecutor, and went to him; I was not quite satisfied with his description of it, and I took it to the office.
NICHOLAS HARVEY re-examined. I believe the prisoner ran by Mr. Maddox's door; I am sure she took my watch- she said she was in distress, and that she lived in Park-street, Oxford-road; I was going to give her a few halfpence out of charity; if I were going to die, she is the woman who took it.
Prisoner's Defence. I heard some one cry out Stop thief! there were two females at a gate, and they asked what was the matter; I said I did not know - the prosecutor took hold of me, and began to pull me about; I said I would not be searched by him.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18290115-233]
501. CHARLES WOOD was indicted for stealing, on the 6th of January, 1 plum-cake, value 2s., the goods of William Marr.
ALEXANDER MARR. My brother, William Marr, is a pastry-cook, and lives in Shoreditch. On Twelfth-night, I was in his parlour, between eleven and twelve o'clock, and my sister called out
"A man has taken something;" I went to the door, and saw the prisoner run away - I pursued him into Dirty-lane, and fell down; I came up to him, and asked what he had taken - he said he had taken nothing; the watchman came up, and we found this cake in the kennel, near where he fell.
JANE MARR. I was at home; I saw the prisoner take the cake out of the window - I called my brother out; I am sure he is the man - he came just inside the door.
JOHN TVRRELL. I was in the shop - I followed the prisoner and Mr. Marr; I took hold of the prisoner,and this cake was picked up just by where he fell.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Recommended to Mercy by the Prosecutor.
Whipped and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18290115-234]
502. ISAAC WATFORD was indicted for stealing on the 13th of January, 1 boot, value 3s., the goods of Joseph Davis.
CHARLOTTE DAVIS. I am the wife of Joseph Davis; he is a cordwainer, and lives in Shoreditch - I was at home on the 13th of January, but was up stairs; I did not see the prisoner till the officer brought him.
ROBERT TYRRELL. I saw the prisoner and another boy on the 13th of January, at half-past three o'clock - the other took a boot from the shop, and gave it to the prisoner, who put it under his coat - they saw me, and ran across the road; I pursued them - the prisoner threw down the boot, and they turned towards Webb-square; I took the prisoner, and brought him back.
JOHN TATUM. I was crossing Shoreditch; the prisoner passed me, and threw down this boot.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 16.
Confined Two Months.
[trialAccount t18290115-235]
503. THOMAS WHITE was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of January, 1 loaf of bread, value 2d.; part of a roasted pig, value 2s.; 8 ozs. weight of butter, value 6d., and 4 French rolls, value 4d., the goods of William Turner.
WILLIAM TURNER. I am a tailor, and live at Bath-place, Kensington. On the 13th of January, we discovered the area had been entered, and these articles taken out of the safe.
THOMAS MAYERS. I am a constable; I live at Queen's-elms. I was in my shop at twenty minutes after eight o'clock on the 12th of January, and saw the prisoner with two large bundles - I followed him twenty yards, and then stopped him; I said, "What have you got there?" he said, "What is that to you?" I said, "I wish to know;" he said he brought them from his father in the creek - I took them off his shoulder; he twisted round, and ran away - I followed some distance, but I had my slippers on, and could not run very fast; in three-quarters of an hour he and his mother came, and wanted to have the property; I said No - I took him to the Magistrate, who ordered him to be confined; the victuals would not keep - I described it to the prosecutor.
WILLIAM TURNER. The description he gave me answered exactly to what I had lost.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18290115-236]
504. THOMAS PEACH was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of December, 1 cheese, value 8s., the goods of John Redding and Thomas Redding.
JOHN REDDING. I am partner with Thomas Redding; we live at Islington-green - we lost a cheese from the top of the pile near the door, on the 13th of December; I was alarmed, ran out, and took the prisoner with it.
THOMAS COPE. I am an officer. I was coming across the green; I saw a mob - the prisoner was in custody, for stealing a cheese - he begged for mercy, and said it was though distress; he seemed in great distress.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
The prisoner pleaded poverty.
GUILTY. Aged 30.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18290115-237]
505. JOHN MORGAN was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of January, 1 cheese, value 10s., the goods of Charles Watts.
JOSEPH SMITH. I am a watchman. On the 13th of January, about seven o'clock, I was in Judd-street; I saw the prisoner go on his hands and knees into Mr. Watts' shop, and take this cheese - he came towards me; I endeavoured to stop him - he passed me; I pursued, and cried Stop thief! I never lost sight of him.
WILLIAM BRIDGER. I am shopman to Charles Watts: this cheese is his; it had been in his shop.
Prisoner's Defence. I was never in the shop - that man has forsworn himself; he would swear a person's life away.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Confined Three Months, and Whipped.
[trialAccount t18290115-238]
506. JOSEPH JONES was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of January, 1 pair of clogs, value 2s. 6d., the goods of Thomas Carew.
MILES HALL. I know Mr. Carew's shop, in Ward's-row, Bethnal-green - he sells clogs. On the 12th of January, I saw the prisoner and another come from the shop, at a quarter before nine o'clock in the evening; the prisoner had something under his jacket - he turned down the first turning; and I saw him stooping behind a pump; he then came towards me - I asked what he had under his jacket; he said Nothing - I went to the pump, and found these clogs at the bottom of it; my brother-officer took the prisoner - we went to the house.
JAMES GLIBBERY. I was with Hall, and took the prisoner; he said another boy gave them to him, and then he said he knew nothing about them - at the officer he said he meant to pawn them.
FRANCES RUSSETT BRETT. I am in the service of Mr. Thomas Carew; I put these clogs on the table in the shop, and when the officer brought them, I identified them.
GUILTY. Aged 14.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18290115-239]
507. JOHN CREWS and DAVID GOGHAN were indicted for stealing, on the 9th of January, 8 books, value 40s., the goods of Martin Doyle.
MARTIN DOYLE. I am a bookseller, and live in High Holborn. On the 9th of January, about half-past six o'clock in the evening, these eight books stood on a pile inside the door - I went into the little room, returned in half a minute, and missed them; the officer brought them in.
WILLIAM COLTON. I am an officer. I was in Holborn, and saw the prisoners and two others walking about the shop; we crossed the road - I saw Crews go up to the door, turn the handle, and open it about six inches -Goghan was looking into the window; Crews then went into the shop, and as we crossed, he was coming out with the books - Goghan was holding the door to let him out, and I laid hold of Crews; he threw the books down in the street - Cadby took Goghan; the other two got away.
JOSEPH CADBY. I was with Colton; what he has stated is correct - I took Goghan.
RICHARD JONES. I was going up Holborn, and saw the prisoners and two others looking at several shops.
CREW'S Defence. I was proceeding along Holborn, and passing the shop, a boy carried some books out in his arms and dropped them; all on a sudden the officer took me and the other prisoner.
GOGHAN's Defence. I was walking past the butter-shop, when the man came and took me.
The prisoners received a good character.
CREWS - GUILTY. Aged 19.
GOGHAN - GUILTY. Aged 19.
Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18290115-240]
508. ANN BARRETT was indicted for stealing, on the 2d of January, 1 silver fork, value 10s., the good of Sarah Allett.
SOPHIA OGDEN. I live with Sarah Allett, my grandmother, in Spring-gardens; she lost a silverfork from off the kitchen table on the 2d of January - we had it for dinner, and I saw it afterwards; the prisoner used to come to the house, and she came that evening, about half-past seven o'clock, to sell oysters; she came into the kitchen - we did not miss the fork till she was brought back; nobody went into the kitchen but the servant.
FREDERICK PRIEST. I am a pawnbroker, and live in Great Queen-street. The prisoner brought this fork on the 2d of January, and asked 1s. 3d. for it; I asked her where she got it - she said a woman who went out charing every day, in Half Moon-street, gave it to her - and then she said a servant in Cecil-street gave it to her; I sent for the officer.
WILLIAM MORGAN. I am the officer. I took the prisoner - she said a person in Cecil-street gave the fork to her, to get some tea and sugar; I said I would go to the place, and then she said she would take me to where she found it in the area.
Prisoner's Defence. As I went to the door, I picked up the fork; I am sorry for it.
GUILTY. Aged 40.
Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18290115-241]
509. MARY ARNOLD was indicted for stealing, on the 15th of January, 1 book, value 2s., the goods of John Poynton.
JOHN POYNTON. I am a bookseller, and live in Russell-court. At a quarter past four o'clock, last Thursday, I saw the prisoner take this book; I followed her, about a dozen yards, and took it from her - she said she merely took it for a lark; there was another woman with her.
Prisoner's Defence. I had not got a yard from the door, and asked if he had got a Vesper book; he said I did not know how to read one, and for my impudence he would give me in charge.
GUILTY. Aged 38.
Confined Fourteen Days.
[trialAccount t18290115-242]
510. JAMES HAYNES was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of January, 1 crown; 8 half-crowns; 10s., and 8 sixpences, the monies of Thomas Stobie.
THOMAS STOBIE. I am a baker, and live in Queen's-buildings, Knightsbridge. On the 16th of January, at a quarter-past five o'clock, I was in the parlour; on looking into the shop, I saw the prisoner on his knees, in the act of taking this money out of the till; it was in a bowl- I took him with it; I had seen it safe two minutes before then - there was 1l. 19s. altogether; he gave it me out of his own hand - he was on his knees inside the counter.
JOSEPH NEWBERRY. I was sent for, and took him.
GUILTY. Aged 15.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18290115-243]
511. WILLIAM CRAIGEE was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of January, 2 shawls, value 5s.; 1 bonnet, value 3s.; 1 veil, value 2s.; 1 purse, value 2d.; 1 ring, value 6d.; 17s. and 1 sixpence; the property of Mary Penny.
MARY PENNY. I am single, and live in Tent-street,Spitalfields; with my mother: I work at the silk business - the prisoner is in the Marines, and lodged in the house- this property was in a box in the room where he slept; it was locked, but the key was in it; he was a friend of ours, and used to come to the house when he had a furlongh; we maintained him without charging him any thing; I missed this property, and 17s. in money, in a purse, on the 1st of January; the prisoner went away that day - he had been with us six weeks; I have seen all the property but the money - upon my oath, I never allowed him to pawn it.
JOSEPH DOWNER. I live at Mr. Kirkhams, a pawnbroker. I have a leghorn bonnet which I took in pawn from Roberts.
MARRY ROBERTS. On the 1st of January, the prisoner brought the bonnet and a bundle of things, and asked me if I would pawn them: I said Yes, not thinking any harm; I pawned this bonnet, twoshawls, a gown, a veil, and other things: I gave the prisoner the money - I had seen him two or three times before; he said the things belonged to his wife; he gave me a new gown.
JOHN TERRY AUSTIN. I am a pawnbroker's assistant. I produce two shawls, and some cotton, pawned by this woman in the name of Roberts.
LAWRENCE NOTLEY. I am a pawnbroker. I have a veil, gown, and shawl, pawned by Roberts on the 1st of January.
ROBERT HALL. I am an officer. I took the prisoner in King-street, Borough; he had this ring on his finger; I found this purse and the duplicates on him.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner. I throw myself on the mercy of the Court.
GUILTY. Aged 24.
[trialAccount t18290115-244]
512. WILLIAM CRAIGEE was again indicted for stealing, on the 1st of January, 1 piece of printed cotton, value 15s.; 1 shawl, value 6s.; 2 gowns, value 6s.; 1 clock, value 3s.; 1 pin, value 3d.; 9 yards of stuff, value 4s., and 1 pair of trousers, value 2s., the goods of John Crawley.
SARAH CRAWLEY. I am the wife of John Crawley. I live in the same house with Mr. Penny; we have the second-floor room, and the prisoner slept on the first- floor; this property was in a box in my room, it was locked, but the key was in it: I and my mother went out on the 1st of January, and left the prisoner in care of the place, when we returned he was gone, and the property.
SAMUEL STEPHENS. I am a pawnbroker. I have a pin pawned with me on the 13th of December, by a man dressed like a soldier - I believe it was the prisoner.
JOHN TERRY AUSTIN. I am a pawnbroker. I have one shawl and this piece of cotton, which belong to this prosecutor.
LAWRENCE NOTLEY. I have one clock and one gown pawned by Roberts.
MARY ROBERTS. I pawned them by the prisoner's direction.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 24.
Transported for Seven Years for each offence.
[trialAccount t18290115-245]
513. THOMAS BENNETT was indicted for stealing, on the 15th of January, 1 sovereign, 2 half-crowns, 5 shillings, 1 sixpence, and 1 farthing, the monies of William Palmer.
WILLIAM PALMER. I am a mason. The prisoner and I lodged in Castle-lane, Westminster when I got up in the morning on the 15th of January, I missed my money which was in my trouser's pocket when I went to bed; the prisoner was in the room when I went to bed, and when I got up; I went out at seven o'clock in the morning to go to my work at the Duke of Wellington's, at Hyde Park-corner, and being a cold morning, I went to have two-penny worth of rum or gin; I found I had no money -I went back, and met my landlord; I told him I had lost my money - he went back with me, and searched the room- the prisoner was then gone, and no other person was there but the landlord's boy, who is ten or eleven years old; the landlord said,
"That man used to lie in bed till ten or eleven o'clock:" we went, and found him with a pipe in his mouth, drinking some purl; he called him out, and said "This man has lost his money, you must know what has become of it:" he said he had not got it - the landlord said, "I shall not be contented - I shall send for an officer," and told me to stand by him; the prisoner kept walking from me - I followed him; he walked faster, and I laid hold of the flap of his coat; my landlord's wife then came up - the prisoner turned, and took my hold from his flap, and then he bolted; she followed him, crying "A thief, stop him!" and I followed him; he was stopped, and brought back - he handed the money over to the landlady- there was a sovereign, two half-crowns, and some small money, making 1l. 10s. 6 1/2d.; he is a labourer, and used to work up at York-house.
JOHN LLOYD. I am the officer. I have 1l. 10s. 6 1/2d., and four duplicates.
Prisoner's Defence. He came home at half-past ten o'clock, and was quite intoxicated; he put his trousers under the head of the bed, and went out in the morning; I was feeling for my stockings, and found the money; when he came to me, he asked if I had the money; I said, in a josular way, that I had not, and walked off.
WILLIAM PALHER re-examined. I did not come home in liquor the night before - I put my trousers under my pillow, and in the morning they were at the top, but I did not consider I had lost the money.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18290115-246]
OLD COURT.
SIXTH DAY. WEDNESDAY, JANUARY 21.
Second London Jury - Before Mr. Recorder.
514. THOMAS SMITH was indicted for stealing, on the 15th of January, 1 coat, value 20s., the goods of Thomas Furneaux.
THOMAS FURNEAUX. I am an officer of the Customs. On the morning of the 15th of January, I was on Cox's-quay, about one o'clock; I took my coat off, and laid it on a cask while I examined my charge and in four minutes my brother officer informed me it was gone - I went on the quay, and he had hold of the prisoner, with the coat.
JAMES GIBBS. I am an officer of the Customs. I was on Cox's-quay, and saw the prisoner about one o'clock in the morning - he was a stranger there; he had nothing inhis possession then; I saw him again in four or five minutes, with the coat on his arm: I asked him about it; he said it was his master's, and that he lived at No.8, Rosemary-lane; I laid hold of him, and said he must give me a better explanation, and in half a minute Furneaux came - he then ran away up a gateway, which was no thoroughfare, and I took him; Furneaux claimed the coat.
Prisoner. Q. I did not run away from him? A. He did; I am certain he told me it was his master's; he afterwards said he had picked it up.
GEORGE HARLEY. I am a watchman - I belong to the quay; I saw the prisoner on the quay, with the coat on his arm - Gibbs was speaking to him; he said the coat was his master's who lived at No.8, Rosemary-lane - I directly collared him: he ran from me, and was secured- he said he was a stranger, and had only come to town that night; and said, if I was a stranger, and picked up a prize in the street, would I not take it.
CHARIES THOMAS TEASDALE. I received him in charge, with the coat.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I had just come to town, and had not money enough to pay for my lodging; I did not mean to take the coat.
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18290115-247]
515. EDWARD BILLETT, THOMAS COOPER, and HENRY BRIGGS, were indicted for stealing, on the 1st of January, 36 yards of carpet, value 25s., the goods of Richard Fawcett and another.
RICHARD FAWCETT. I have one partner - we keep a carpet warehouse in Bishopsgate-street. On the 1st of January I was absent, when the carpet was taken; I saw it at the Mansion-house next day-there were thirty-six yards, which are worth 25s.
JOSEPH FRVER. I am a Bow-street patrol. On the 1st of January I saw all the three prisoners together, and watched them; I first saw half an hour- I saw them standing against Mr. Fawcett's window for about twenty minutes; Briggs got on Cooper's shoulder. and they all three came together from the end of the window to the door - Briggs then pulled this carpet down off a high shelf in the shop; he could not have reached it unless he had been on the other's shoulder - Billett stood against the door at the time, and had been in their company all the time; Priggs pulled the carpet down - Billett caught it in his arms, and ran across the road with it; I secured him with it in his possession: he threw it down, and tried to get away; the other two ran up Bishopsgate-street, in different direction - I knew them before: I saw them in Shoreditch about half an hour after; they had then changed their coats; it happened at half-past four o'clock.
JOHN ROBERTSON. I am a constable of Shoreditch. I saw the three prisoners in company, about four o'clock, within two hundred yards of the prosecutor's shop; in about half an hour I received information from Fryer, that he wanted Cooper and Briggs, and we apprehended them - I said, "Why, Cooper, you have got Briggs' coat on, and Briggs has got your jacket on;" he denied it at first, but at the watch-house they again shifted their clothes.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
BILLETT'S Defence. I had been over the water, and as I returned I saw two lads at this door knock the carpet down - I took it up, and walked over the road.
BRIGGS' Defence. I was not with these lads; I had not seen them for an hour and a half before.
BILLETT- GUILTY. Aged 21.
COOPER- GUILTY. Aged 17.
BRIGGS- GUILTY. Aged 18.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18290115-248]
516. ORLANDO DOUGLAS TALVER was indicted for embezzlement.
JOHN HOOD. I live in Temple-street, and am a baker. The prisoner was in my employ, and was entrusted to receive money for me; he came into my service in June. -Rouse was a customer of mine; he never paid me this money received from Rouse - he should settle with me every night.
JAMES ROUSE. I keep the Blue Last public-house, Dorset-street,Salisbury-square. On the 15th of December the prisoner brought my bread, which came to 1s. 6d., a little before eight o'clock in the morning; I paid him for it - I had more bread from him the following day, and paid 1s., on the 16th, and another 1s. on the 17th; Mr. Hood afterwards sent me a bill of 5l. 6s. 9d - I had paid it all; I stated that I had paid the prisoner, who had then absconded.
JOHN HOOD. The prisoner left me a week before I discovered this; he went away on one Monday, and the next Monday the bill was delivered - he left without giving notice; I knew him before he came to live with me.
THOMAS ROBINSON. I am a constable. The prisoner was brought to the watch-house on the 29th of December, for embezzling 14l. - I found only 7 1/2d. on him.
Prisoner. I am very sorry, but I did not intend to keep it.
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18290115-249]
517. JOHN JONES was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of January, 2 sheets of brown paper, value 1d.; 120 copy-books, value 3l. 10s.; 10 quires of paper, called quarto post, value, 10s., and 2 quires of note paper, value 1s., the goods of Louisa Dibbin.
SECOND COUNT, stating it to belong to James Barry and another.
HENRY DIBBIN. I am the son of Louisa Dibbin, proprietor of Gerards' Hall inn, Basing-lane . On the 17th of January I saw the prisoner brought into the yard, with this parcel, by the book-keeper: the prisoner is a stranger.
HENRY DALBY. I am book-keeper at Gerards' Hall. On the 17th of January I met the prisoner coming out of the yard with this parcel, carrying it in front of him - it weighed nearly half a cwt.; he had not got quite outside the yard - he was a stranger: he passed me, and went down Basing-lane; I ran into the office, and then ran and caught him in Watling-street, with it; I had seen the parcel inside the warehouse three minutes before: I asked where he got it - he told me to let him go; he would not go with me - I was obliged to carry him; I found the parcel entered in our books - it was to go to Gravesend: Mr. Dibbin had entered it, and the book is here. GEORGE TAYLOR. I picked up this parcel, and sent it to Gerards' Hall - it contained the articles stated in the indictment, and belonged to James Barry and another.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
The prisoner pleaded distress.
GUILTY. Aged 43.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18290115-250]

ROBERT ROBERTS was indicted for stealing, on the 15th of November, 1 tea-caddy, value 10s.; 1 watch, value 15s.; 1 handkerchief, value 3s.; 2 glass jugs, value 20s.; 1 stuffed bird and a glass case, value 15s.; 1 dish, value 2s.; 1 japanned bread-tray, value 1s. 6d., and 1 cannister, value 2s. 6d., the goods of Mary Ann Chandler.

NEW COURT, SIXTH DAY.
Fifth Middlesex Jurn - Before Mr. Sergeant Arabin.
519. WILLIAM JOHNSON was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of January, 1 jacket, value 30s.; 1 pair of trouseres, value 15s.; 1 waistcoat, value 10s., and 1 shirt, value 8s., the goods of George Downie.
GEORGE DOWNIE. I am a sailor, and live with Mr. Briant. On the 8th of January I lost these clothes from a chest by my bedside; I put them there at night, and in the morning, when I awoke, they were all gone - I never saw the prisoner; I was asleep before he came in, and when he went out.
GEORGE BRIANT. I keep the Boy and Helmet public-house, in East Smithfield; Downie lodged with me for about three weeks - on the 8th he went to bed, and the watchman brought the prisoner in quite late, and asked if I could give him a lodging; I led him up to bed - he asked what there was to pay; I said, "You are a poor man, 6d."I went into Downie's room to get a candle: told him to put it out, and bid him good night; the next morning he got up early, knocked at my door, and asked me to let him out - I got up in the dark and let him out: about seven o'clock the prosecutor came down, and said he had lost his clothes - the prisoner was afterwards found on boards a ship, but none of the property on him; no other persons slept in the house.
Prisoner. I went into the room you shewed me; I put on my own clothes in the morning - I had no bundle or any thing with me. Witness. I let him out with the key, which I always take with me; I did not see him with any bundle, or that he looked more bulky than the night before.
ROBERT HENRY BURGESS. I took the prisoner. I have two old shirts and a pair of old trousers, which were found in the room which the prisoner slept in that night - a key and a handkerchief were found in the prisoner's bed, which had been in the prosecutor's jacket pocket.
Prisoner's Defence. He took me on board a ship, just as I was coming on board; I never saw these things in my life, which are said to have been found in my bed.
GEORGE BRIANT. No one else slept in the house that night; they might possibly have been there before.
GUILTY. Aged 38.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18290115-252]
520. JOHN OSBORN was indicted for stealing, on the 9th of January, 1 cap, value 3s. the goods of Joel Hotchkiss.
THOMAS WISEMAN. I keep the Prince Regent public-house, in Ratcliff-highway; the prosecutor Joel Hotchkiss, who is now gone to sea, and another man, slept in a doublebedded-room, on the 9th of January, in my house; the prosecutor came down and said he had lost his cap - I told him it was not possible, as no one had left the house, and I told him to go and look under the clothes; he did so, but could not find it - on the Saturday following I went to the Anchor and Hope public-house, and found the prisoner there intoxicated, and to my great surprise he had this cap on his head, which I knew to be the prosecutor's, as I had had it in my possession before, and I knew it by this mark.
Prisoner. Q. Did not 1 lose a pair of trousers that night? A. You said you did, and your waistcoat; I went and got you a pair.
JAMES FOGG. I took the prisoner, and produce the cap.
Prisoner's Defence. I bought the cap of a man that came from St. Mary-axe, and the publican took it off my head, because he thought I would not pay him a little money.
GUILTY. Aged 11.
Confined Six Weeks.
[trialAccount t18290115-253]
521. JOHN SMITH and JAMES HILBERT were indicted for stealing, on the 11th of January, 4 carriage glasses, value 4l., the goods of Watkin William Martin.
WILLIAM SUMMERFIELD. I am a watchman of Mary-le-bone. On the 11th of January, at a quarter before seven o'clock in the morning, I was in Adam-street, Westminster - I watched the premises of Mr. Martin, a stable-keeper, in consequence of information, and saw the two prisoners cross Upper Berkley-street, which is two or three hundred yards from Mr. Martin's - they were in company, and coming towards me; I am positive they are the two men: Smith was carrying something - I went and clapped my hand upon his shoulder, and said,
"You are the very fellow I want;" I took these eight glasses from under his arm - Hilbert immediately decamped; I called for assistance and gave Smith into custody - Hilbert ran into Upper Seymour-street; I ran and took him as he was stooping as if going to tie his shoe - I am confident he was with Smith.
WILLIAM GOROON. I am a gas-lighter. On Sunday morning, the 11th of January, at a quarter before seven o'clock, I saw the two prisoners in the news and gave information to the watchman - Smith asked me what o'clock it was.
WATKINS WILLIAM MARTIN. I am a liverystablekeeper - my stable is in the mews; these four glasses are my property - they were in post-chaises which were standing in the mews; I have no doubt they are mine - one is broken and the piece of it is left behind; they would cost me about 8l.
SMITH'S Defence. I was passing by the mews, and saw two young men pass with these glasses under their arms- they went to a watch-box, returned and placed them against the side of the mews, and when the watchman came by, they went to a watch-box, returned and placed them against the side of the mews, and when the watchman came by, they decamped; I staid till be passed, and then I must own I was going to take away the glasses.
SMITH - GUILTY. Aged 23.
HILBERT - GUILTY. Aged 17.
[trialAccount t18290115-254]
522. JOHN SMITH and JAMES HILBERT were again indicted for stealing, on the 11th of January, 4 carriage glasses, value 4l., the goods of Edward Kirkley.
WILLIAM SUMMERFIELD. I am a watchman. Mr. Kirkley is a livery stable-keeper, in Cumberland-mews North, near to Williams'- mews; I received information a quarter before seven o'clock on the morning of the 11th of January, and saw the prisoners two or three hundred yards from the mews; I took the glasses from Smith, and said,"You are the very lad I am looking for;" I gave him into custody, then pursued Hilbert, who had run away, and took him.
WILLIAM GORDON. I saw the prisoner in Williams'-mews, Adam-street, not far from Mr. Kirkley's premises. I gave information to the watchman.
EDWARD KIRKLEY. I am a livery stable-keeper -These four glasses are mine; I can be quite positive of two of them, by some lace on the frame, which matches the lace on the chaise - I had lent my chaise to Mr. Martin the day before, and they were on his premises, next to his chaises: they are worth 8l.
SMITH - GUILTY. Aged 23.
HILBERT - GUILTY. Aged 17.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18290115-255]
523. CATHERINE WILLIAMS and ELIZABETH SULLIVAN were indicted for stealing, on the 12th of January, 2 coats, value 3l.; 1 pair of boots, value 12s.; 1 pair of trousers, value 6s.; 1 waistcoat, value 5s.; 1 bat, vlaue 7s., and 3 handkerchiefs, value 10s., the goods of George Eastham.
GEORGE EASTHAM. I am a tailor, and live in Church-place, Somer's-town. On the 12th of January I lost the articles stated in St. Giles'; the two prisoners caught me in the street, and carried me to their apartments; I was drunk- the clothes were on my back: I awoke about four o'clock, in the prisoners' apartments - some of my clothes were stopped upon them.
THOMAS HOBBS. I am a watchman of Whitechapel. At half-past two o'clock on the morning of the 13th of January, I met the two prisoners; I followed them to Rosemary-lane, where I called another watchman - we went and took them: I asked what they had got - they said, clothes belonging to their husbands, and they had taken them out of pawn; I said I did not believe it, being that time in the morning: the things were under their shawls - I took a hat and a close body coat from Williams.
THOMAS FOLKER. Hobbs called me to his assistance, and I secured Sullivan - she said she had got her husband's coat, and I found this light drab coat on her.
THOMAS BROWN. I took charge of the prisoners. I found this handkerchief in Sullivan's pocket, and this other was in the great coat pocket.
GEORGE EASTHAM. These are my property. I had been spending the day in town, and had no memory; I met the prisoners in Oxford-street - I me Sullivan first, who asked me to treat her; I refused at first, but I did treat her - another woman was with her, but I cannot tell whether it was the other prisoner.
SULLIVAN'S Defence. We were coming home, and met with this parcel at a door - we took it, and the watchmen detained us; we were making the best of our way home.
WILLIAMS - GUILTY. Aged 19.
SULLIVAN - GUILTY. Aged 20.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18290115-256]
524. JAMES LOVE was indicted for stealing, on the 4th of July, I mustard-pot, value 4l.; 3 salt-cellars, value 2l.; I decanter, value 10s., and 1 basket, value 6d., the goods of William Carter, his master.
WILLIAM CARTER. I am a China and glass-seller. -The prisoner was my errand-boy; he had been with me a week or ten days - I delivered him these artices on the 4th of July, to take to Messrs. Pellatt and Green's glass-house, to have glass linings to them, and a decanter to the stand; they were silver - he never returned: I never found him till lately, when he was taken on another charge.
WILLIAM MYNER. I am warehouse clerk to Messrs. Pellatt and Green. This property was not brought to my employors'; if they had I should have known it.
WILLIAM JUDGE. I am an officer. I apprehended the prisoner for robbing his master, the Captain of a vessel.
GUILTY. Aged 14.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18290115-257]
525. THOMAS CALVER was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of January, 1 umbrella, value 7s., the goods of Alexander Smyth, from his person.
ALEXANDER SMYTH. I live in Mark-lane. On the 14th of January I was coming from Covent-garden Theatre; I walked as far as Norfolk-street - Mrs. Smyth had hold of my arm, and I had my umbrella in my right hand; I felt a severe pull at it, but I did not let it go: presently I felt a more severe pull, which bent my wrist, opened my hand, and got away my umbrella; I pursued the prisoner - a gentleman followed, and took him: I came up, and he was delivered up to me - I gave him in charge; I am certain he is the man.
WILLIAM JOHN McBEATH. I was on my way from Waterloo-bridge to Norfolk - street; I saw this gentleman and his lady followed by the prisoner - I saw the Strand; which got the umbrella from him; the gentleman called out that he had robbed him of his umbrella: I followed the prisoner, and took him by the New church in the Strand; I am positive he is the man.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Transported for Seven Years.
